   The listener's bounds are the operator's and the replica's. `serve` holds the directory's lock, so a `relay publish`
   there is `BUSY`.
3. *One credential per relay (`relay.token`), never per holder:* one per holder would link that holder's acts, which a
   third party's relay exists to part. It gates what the relay pays for: the relay judges a file's frame, not its
   validity (that needs the backing's evidence and proofs), and every framed record costs it a fee and a box minimum.
   `relay.json` names the URL and the credential to hand to holders.
4. *`moe relay send <file> --to <relay.json>`* opens no directory: it checks the file and posts it, to an onion URL only
   through the holder's own loopback proxy (refused `PROXY` before any connection otherwise, M12a's check), and prints
   the relay's answer, checked to name the file's record, or its refusal's code; a relay that does not answer is
   `UNAVAILABLE`, and the send is repeated. Its 20-second bound passes the listener's 15, so a publication still in
   flight there usually ends as a reply that did not come (an onion circuit's setup counts against the client alone).
5. *Explanations:* every publication file `publish` and `publish-acceptance` write, and the first notes, say what a
   relay's funding links, how to take `relay.json` and when to try a second relay. The visibility guide closes duty 2
   with its limits and adds the relay to the parties table; the wallet guide's Relays section gives the recipe.
6. *Duty 3 (syncs) stands as M12a and M12b left it:* the onion transport with isolation per command, the service-wide
   credential, credential-free replicas; a command that both syncs and submits shows the operator its backing on one
   circuit (recipe: `sync`, then `pay --package`, with separate credentials), and replicas are read after the operator.

**Rationale:**
- *A relay directory per demand alone* (no code): links only that demand's acts, but each key must be funded, and the
  reference cannot make coins with no history; funding the keys from one source links them again. Kept as the fallback.
- *Sponsored funding by an Ergo contract* (a box anyone may spend into a kind-4 output): a venue mechanism the adopted
  venue-ergo context does not name, and a script cannot judge a publication, so anyone drains it with framed garbage.
- *Paying the relay in the pool:* nothing is admitted in a gap; prepaying links the holder to the relay beforehand.
  Sponsored holder funding stays in Next 9.
- *K or the operator funds:* the operator is absent in a gap; K funding the demands against it is a conflict.
- *Blind-signed one-use relay tokens:* a new primitive (AGENTS.md); one shared credential suffices for the smallest profile.
- *An open relay (no credential):* a stranger drains it; a relay meaning to be open publishes its credential.
- *The route inside the replica's `reader serve`:* would put a funding key in an evidence role that holds none; a relay
  and a replica can share one onion name by port.
- *A mixer* for per-demand keys: outside the protocol, a deployment's choice.

**Evidence:** `test/pool-v3-service-http.test.ts` (relay listener: credential form, the one route under the one
credential, malformed and oversized bodies, named refusals and the rest `UNAVAILABLE` told to the owner, a request
reading as gone once answered; the client's URL and credential forms and the relay's refusal code). The lit command
drill (`npm run check:lit:commands`): with the operator stopped past silence, the holder hands its gap demand and its
release to a third party's `relay serve` behind its own onion name with `relay send` through the CONNECT proxy only
(its guard admits no other connection), an exact resend answers the same transaction, both read final by force; a send
without the proxy (`PROXY`), with a wrong credential (`UNAUTHORIZED`) and for another venue (`VENUE`) are refused; the
publication notes name the relay's funding; the backer's acceptance goes through its own relay. A fresh review of the
proposal found no blocker and three majors, all taken: the notes said a third party's relay links nothing to the holder,
false where a hostile relay hands holders distinct `relay.json` files (now stated, with the recipe, and the note says
what the act is tied to); the budget bounds loss, not availability, against any credential holder (stated); the request
queue was unbounded and work queued for a lost connection still spent (one waiting request, gone requests dropped,
listening after a first sync). Its minors, taken: handing one file to two relays at once fingerprints the pair (recipe:
a second only after a bound); the timing limit is the sync `publish` makes just before the file, not the next; the
budget counts each rebuilt transaction (stated); a failed publisher now ends `serve` and the lock is stated; the funding
key is hot (fund to the budget). Nits taken: `EARLY`'s retry documented; the alternatives above.
A fresh adversarial review of the integrated patch confirmed the listener's order (loopback, exactly one credential,
constant-time compare, route, bounded body; duplicate and lower-case credentials refused by raw socket), the `gone`
predicate after a peer's FIN and RST, serialized turns with no refusal leaking between requests, `relay publish`'s
unchanged refusal order and output, and the client refactor's identical behaviour. It found one major, taken: the
pool-v3 command drill still counted three first notes (now four, the relay note asserted). Minors taken: a venue that
has witnessed nothing yet ended `serve` instead of waiting for its first sync; the client's bound passes the
listener's only usually; the publisher's permanent refusals read as retryable `UNAVAILABLE` (now `TOO_LARGE`, `FULL`);
the queue bound's wording; no test of `serve`'s turns, refusal codes or the send's parsers (`test/relay.test.ts`, and a
duplicate credential on the relay listener). Nits taken: `--port` with Tor's `HiddenServicePort`; a resend answers a
new transaction after a rebuild; `EARLY` passes once the relay's nodes reach the instant. A publisher failing to
persist ending `serve` with `STORAGE` is not exercised by a test.

**Limits:** the relay sees each act a little before the public and may delay or withhold it. A relay can tell holders
apart by handing out distinct `relay.json` files. Anyone holding the credential can spend the budget on framed garbage
and stop the relay for everyone until refunded (a budget per window of witnessed indices, or a check that a release
names a witnessed demand, are levers if a deployment needs them). The relay's users are one anonymity set only as
large as its traffic, and timing pairs an act with the sync before it. Who runs and funds public relays is a
deployment's matter. Tor is assumed and not measured here (Open question).

**Spec change:** none needed.

## 2026-10-07 — Serve kept, verified evidence from a replica with no credential, and keep the operator's credential service-wide (slice 12 M12b)

**Status:** accepted after review; delivery is in WORK.md.

**Question:** slice 12 acceptance (b) and (c) ([direction of 2026-10-07](#2026-10-07--exercise-lits-failure-path-and-settle-7s-acceptance-owner-before-adoption-and-give-evidence-availability-a-replica-in-slice-12)
item 2). Only the operator's `serve` supplied evidence. A holder who missed the last sync, or a fresh reader, had no source
while the operator was down, and the gate "available, recoverable state" asks for evidence from independently operated
sources. Construction: "Published means retrievable by a stranger"; pool-v3 §14 has a holder obtain the trail from
replicas in a gap (C2b.3.3). The evidence read took the service-wide wallet token, and duty 3 asked for a credential that
is not per holder. How does a reader serve what it kept and verified, through the one wire, incrementally, without
trusting what its own suppliers say about it?

**Decision:**
1. *`moe reader serve` makes a reader directory a replica.* Each round it syncs the view, then each kept backing's
   evidence from its sources into `evidence.db`, and reads the frontier as `supply` does (proofs verified). Where that
   read finds the source's selection canonical at the replica's witnessed index, the replica serves that selection with
   the read's own package (the configuration and the operator-signed commitment; sequence, operator and root equal to
   its read's canonical commitment). It serves `GET /evidence` through the
   served frame and route the operator's service uses (`createV3EvidenceService`), from what `evidence.db` holds, so it
   serves while the operator is down and across its own restarts. It signs and admits nothing. It writes `replica.json`
   and prints a line when listening, one per backing whose served sequence moved or whose read was not served, and one
   when stopped; a round's refusal, the holder's missing proxy included, and a stream its storage cut short are events.
   It holds its directory's lock while it runs: terms and replicas are added with it stopped.
2. *No credential on a replica.* The route alone; every other request is `NOT_FOUND`; a credential sent is ignored and
   the client sends none. Bounds are the operator's (loopback peers, sixteen connections, eight streams, a minimum
   rate); behind Tor it is an onion service like the holders' listener.
3. *The operator's wallet credential stays one per service,* handed with the URL to every holder and never per holder,
   so it identifies nobody; a deployment may publish it beside its terms. It bounds use of the operator's connections,
   not who reads published evidence. No code change.
4. *Incremental serving by the replica's own index.* A replica's evidence file (opened `shared`, WAL) records each
   directory, snapshot and trail top it takes per operator, pending, in the transaction that keeps it, and assigns the
   pending ones to the next selection it serves above every one of that operator served before (`keepSelection`),
   whose read was final over what the file holds; at the highest already served only the backing's selection moves, and
   below it nothing is kept. Asked after a sequence, it serves what it assigned to later selections up to the served one,
   and every trail top so assigned that no other served top, or the furthest top the reader already holds, reaches, so a
   longer fork a supplier sent never displaces the trail the replica's read used. A reader served through one of this
   replica's selections holds everything the file held then, so it is served everything kept since; a mark above every
   selection the file served came from a file it no longer is, and is served from nothing. No supplier's selection
   places an object. The read's own package must be the configuration and the commitment alone, so nothing a source
   puts in it is read and not kept.
5. *Each replica keeps its own mark in a reader's store* (a client with no credential keys its mark by URL); the
   operator's service keeps one mark at any URL, as before. A source whose selection is below the mark leaves it.
6. *Holders read the operator first and replicas where it does not answer, refuses or sends evidence that does not
   frame or assemble* (or where it keeps no service file); where no replica answers, the operator's refusal stands.
   `replica add <backing> <url>` keeps them per operator in order. A replica that fails likewise is passed over and
   named (`skipped`); a damaged object ends the replica's stream, so the reader
   keeps its mark; the holder's own missing proxy still refuses `PROXY`, a replica's reply naming `PROXY` is its
   refusal. Each read prints its `evidence` source. Duty 3's sync and submit are parted by the transport (M12a): `sync`,
   then `pay --package`, on separate circuits.
7. *Acceptance:* the lit command drill runs a replica behind its own onion name; with the operator stopped past silence,
   the holder's demand reads the replica's evidence (the operator passed over), and a reader that never synced and keeps
   no operator service reads its supply final from the replica alone, past a replica that is down; with the operator
   back the holder reads its service first and the replica follows the return.

**Rationale:**
- *Tags from each upstream stream's selection,* the first proposal: a reader's mark shared across sources let a
  withholding replica raise it so the honest operator never resent what was withheld, and a hostile or lagging upstream
  could place objects below the sequence that signed them, so a chained replica would never serve them. Assigning
  objects at the replica's own final reads needs no supplier's statement.
- *Tags derived from held commitments* (commitment root to directory to snapshots and openings): exact, but reimplements
  the reader's walk for an index.
- *Replicas first,* which keeps the operator from joining a holder's sync to its submission: a replica that lags or
  withholds while the operator is up would leave reads unresolved with no fall-through, unless every command retried
  its read per source. The transport already parts sync from submission; replicas carry availability.
- *A mark per URL for every source:* the operator's loopback URL changes at each restart (port 0), costing a whole sync.
- *A static mirror of whole packages,* *a new wire field,* *a journal in a mirror mode,* *a separate index database*
  (no shared transaction with the kept parts) and *a token on the replica* (a stranger cannot retrieve) were refused.
- *Index in every store:* a wallet would pay rows and a whole resync after upgrade for a role it never serves; only a
  file once opened `shared` indexes, and clears its suppliers' marks once so its next sync is whole.

**Evidence:** `test/replica.test.ts` (lit journal behind the service, a replica's file and listener): a wallet paid and
credited through the replica, the payee from nothing; after a mark, only what the replica kept since (fewer items, one
trail); nothing new until the replica serves a later selection; selections only rise; another operator's parts wait
for that operator's own selection; the served reads return with the file; `NOT_FOUND` for commands, `STALE` for a
backing it does not serve, a credential ignored and never sent; damage ends the stream and the reader keeps its mark; a
file kept before the index is cleared to a whole sync; `PROXY` from a replica passed over, the holder's own not.
`test/pool-v3-service-client.test.ts`: the operator's mark at any URL, a replica's its own. The lit command drill
passes as item 7 states. `npm run check:pool:v3-service` passes with the operator restarted on new ports.
`test/replica.test.ts` also covers what a source ahead sent filed only under a later selection, and the trail the
read used served beside a longer fork.
A fresh review of the first proposal found one blocker and six majors, all resolved: a source that answers ended the
search though its evidence left the read unresolved (operator first, replicas as fallback, item 6); supplier-placed tags
trusted every upstream and only fell (the replica's own index, item 4); tags without an operator (per operator); the
served selection ambiguous under a rising mark and kept outside `evidence.db` (in the file, set only by a final read);
damaged objects silently left out (the stream ends); a remote `PROXY` code read as the holder's own (status 0 only).
Minors taken: per-stream read connections, never a second store on the file; the index only in replica files; the
operator's mark kept across URLs. Recorded below as limits: client deadlines, storage and egress, duty 3's ordering.
A fresh adversarial review of the integrated patch, with reproducers, found one blocker and two majors, all taken: a
round reading an earlier selection final again filed what a source ahead had sent under a sequence readers already
held (selections rise strictly per operator); a longer fork from a hostile supplier displaced the trail the replica's
read used (every unreached top served); an operator that refused rather than went silent kept holders from every
replica (refusals pass to replicas). Its minors taken: a mark from a lost file served from nothing; a source's own
package carrying more than its configuration and commitment refused; the served commitment's root compared; the
holder's missing proxy a round event in `serve`; a stream cut by storage damage an event; the index made atomically;
comments, the line protocol's wording, a malformed replicas file as `INVALID` and the damage test's refusal. Left as
limits: a replica's own file damage is not repaired by a whole sync; `SERVICE_URL` takes ports outside 1–65535
(pre-existing form, the connection refuses).

**Limits:** a replica serves only what its own sources supplied and its read verified; one the holder lists that
withholds while the operator is down leaves reads unresolved until the operator answers or another replica is listed,
since a read is not retried per source. A replica's selection lags the operator's by its round and the venue's
finality. A listed source can add up to the batch quota to a holder's evidence file at each sync (as an operator can),
and the client has no overall deadline (Next 4 (m)). Storage damage in a replica's file ends its streams until a copy
supplied again repairs it; rounds never sync whole. Token-free egress is bounded only per connection: anyone can stream whole histories,
eight at a time. A served stream holds one WAL read snapshot for its length, so a slow reader grows the WAL until it
ends. No live Tor run (Open question).

**Spec change:** none needed (pool-v3 §14 and C2b.3.3 already assume replicas; no byte, identity or verdict changes).

## 2026-10-07 — Reach an operator as an onion service through the holder's own proxy, and serve holders on a listener of their own (slice 12 M12a)

**Status:** accepted after review; delivery is in WORK.md.

**Question:** slice 12's first probe ([2026-10-03](#2026-10-03--measure-the-design-point-at-sizes-a-run-can-prove-and-give-the-holders-transport-and-funding-a-slice-before-release-assurance)
item 2): can the wallet's, relay's and reader's clients reach the service and nodes through a user-chosen anonymising
proxy with no new cryptography, and at what cost? Visibility duty 3 asks for a transport for submissions and syncs that
does not identify the holder; Construction §C1.4 assumes an anonymising transport without supplying one. The service
client took `http://127.0.0.1` only, so no holder could reach an operator on another machine at all.

**Decision:**
1. *The transport is the holder's own proxy, through Node's environment proxy.* Every client in `src/` speaks HTTP through
   `fetch`; with `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1` (or `--use-env-proxy`) and `HTTP_PROXY`, Node 24 tunnels each request by HTTP
   `CONNECT`, which Tor's `HTTPTunnelPort` serves. The reference adds no SOCKS client, dependency or cryptography.
2. *An operator outside the holder's machine is a Tor onion service* (`http://<v3 host>/`, the host's form checked; Tor
   checks its checksum): the address authenticates the service key and encrypts the path, where a clearnet `http:` URL
   would carry the bearer credential in clear. Clearnet `https:` stays refused until a deployment needs a TLS policy.
3. *The client refuses `PROXY` before any connection* unless `fetch` will tunnel the onion URL through a loopback `http:`
   proxy, judged as Node's bundled undici (7.29.1) judges it: the global dispatcher is its `EnvHttpProxyAgent`;
   `http_proxy ?? HTTP_PROXY` names a loopback `http:` proxy (a remote one could answer for the onion and take the
   token); `no_proxy ?? NO_PROXY` does not exempt the host under undici's matching (entries split on commas and
   whitespace, a lone `*`, suffixes, ports). An onion name therefore never reaches a resolver. A proxy that refuses the
   connection is `PROXY` too, not an operator that did not answer (which would read kept evidence). An onion client
   takes no admin credential.
4. *Holders get a listener of their own.* Behind an onion service every peer is the loopback Tor daemon, so the loopback
   check no longer keeps the network out, and the admin commands and the sixteen connections would face anyone with the
   name. A service created without an admin credential serves submission and evidence only; `moe operator serve --onion
   <host> [--holder-port <p>]` runs one beside the operator's, with its own connections, and writes `holders.json`.
5. *Isolation is per command.* Each `moe` command is one process over one backing, and Tor isolates streams by the proxy
   credential (`IsolateSOCKSAuth`, on by default; Node sends the URL's credentials as `Proxy-Authorization`), so a
   credential per command keeps commands on separate circuits. The wallet guide gives the recipe and its limit.
6. *Acceptance:* the lit command drill runs every holder process (wallet, reader, relay) against the operator only by an
   onion name, through a CONNECT-only proxy that maps the name to the holders' listener as Tor does, refuses every other
   target and answers `502` where the listener is down; the node stays direct (`NO_PROXY=127.0.0.1`). A preloaded guard
   ends any holder process that connects anywhere but the proxy and the node, and every wallet `sync` and `fulfill` while
   the service is up must read served evidence. Hostile cases: no proxy and a proxy that is down (`PROXY`), and
   `--holder-port` without or with a malformed `--onion` (usage). The pool-v3 command drill stays direct.

**Rationale:**
- *A SOCKS5 client in the reference* (an undici `Agent` with its own connect): duplicates Tor's HTTP tunnel and the
  environment proxy, with a dispatcher threaded through every client. Node 24's own `socks5:` proxy URLs work but are
  experimental and print a warning to the stderr agents read; refused for now.
- *An undici dependency for in-process, per-backing isolation:* a dependency and per-call plumbing, where every command
  already touches one backing.
- *Clearnet `https:` with pinning:* a TLS policy and certificates, and the operator learns the holder's address.
- *A local forwarder per operator* (socat over Tor) in front of the loopback-only client: every holder runs one, and
  nothing stops a misconfigured one from going direct.
- *Exposing the operator's one listener through Tor:* opens `commit` and `publish` to the network behind the admin token,
  and lets an unauthenticated client hold the sixteen connections the operator's own commands need.

**Evidence:** the probe (disposable, Intel Xeon @ 2.10GHz, Node 24.21.0) ran the whole lit command drill with every
process through a CONNECT-only proxy: passed, 263 tunnels, 127.7 s against 122.8 s direct (one run each); credentials
in the proxy URL arrive as `Proxy-Authorization`. It also found that undici 7.29.1 retries, about 6,000 times a second,
a CONNECT whose proxy closes the socket without a status line, until the request's deadline (10 s for the service, 30 s
for a node supplier, 60 s for the publisher); Tor and the drill's proxy answer `502`. `test/service-onion.test.ts`: 20
environments, each run in a fresh Node process whose fetch of an onion URL either tunnels through a proxy or does not,
with the check accepting exactly where it tunnels; remote, `https:` and `socks5:` proxies refused; undici's `NO_PROXY`
matching; malformed onion hosts; an onion client given an admin credential; the refusal before any connection. A
holders' listener refuses commit and publish, the admin credential included (`test/pool-v3-service-http.test.ts`). The lit
command drill passes as item 6 states (153 processes, 125 s).
A fresh review of the proposal found three majors, all taken: the first check, by variable, accepted cases where fetch
goes direct (an empty `http_proxy` hiding `HTTP_PROXY`, `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=true`, `--no-use-env-proxy`, `NO_PROXY`
forms), now judged as undici does and tested against real processes; per-process isolation does not part a command's
sync from its submission (item 5's limit, with the recipe); exposing the one listener opened admin and its connections
to the Tor network (item 4). Its minors: a drill that could pass through a kept-evidence fallback or a direct node fetch
(the guard and the served assertion); `socks5:` works in Node 24 (recorded above); a remote proxy could impersonate the
service (refused); a proxy that is down read as an unanswered operator (`PROXY`); the parameter download at `init` and a
malformed `HTTP_PROXY` (documented). Nits: the onion form's version character, a trailing dot, `service add` checking
the URL form (all taken). A fresh adversarial review of the integrated patch found no
way past the proxy and no admin command on the holders' listener: it checked the judgement against undici line by line
(precedence, empty values, a lone star, splitting, suffixes, ports, normalised proxy hosts), mutated the oracle (each
mutation fails a case), and probed two Authorization headers, the admin token and `null` credentials on the listener,
both listeners' closing, and an onion that is down behind a live proxy (a `502`, read as unanswered, so a holder in a
gap reads kept evidence). Taken: two oracle cases cannot arise on Windows, whose environment is case-insensitive, and
are skipped there (major, the Windows CI job); a proxy named `localhost` with two addresses, or `[::1]` without IPv6,
failed as a connection without a port and read as an unanswered operator, now any connection failure of an onion
request (Tor answers an onion's failure with a status) and drilled with `localhost`; the guard let `node:http`'s
`path: null` connections through; the served-evidence assertion missed the drill's polling reads; nits (the service
guide's credentials, the documented command, a `listen` error handler left attached, a stale `holders.json`).

**Limits:** no live Tor here (the container has none): latency, onion rendezvous isolation by credential and Tor's
refusal of internal addresses are read from the Tor manual, not measured; a local-machine run is an Open question. The
service credential is still service-wide and handed out of band (M12b, with the replica). The onion listener's
connections are open to anyone who knows the name (Tor's `HiddenServicePoWDefensesEnabled` and
`HiddenServiceMaxStreams`). undici's retry loop on a silent proxy is bounded by deadlines only. Gap funding (duty 2)
is M12c.

**Spec change:** none needed (Construction §C1.4 assumes the transport; no byte, identity or verdict changes).

## 2026-10-07 — Adopt moe/lit/v1 with §9's configuration, reading the contracts in the text pool-v3 fixes (slice 14 M14h)

**Status:** accepted after review; specification merged (money-from-first-principles #18, `80a4ea1`).

**Question:** slice 14 ordered lit-v1's adoption last ([M14f](#2026-10-06--serve-a-lit-scope-from-the-one-operator-journal-the-construction-an-option-slice-14-m14f)
item 4; [direction of 2026-10-07](#2026-10-07--exercise-lits-failure-path-and-settle-7s-acceptance-owner-before-adoption-and-give-evidence-availability-a-replica-in-slice-12)
item 1), once the commands had run lit's failure path (M14g4), §7's acceptance owner was settled (M14g5) and C3.8 read lit
answers (M14g5b). Adoption fixes every byte, identity and verdict of `moe/lit/v1` (a later change is `moe/lit/v2` by
successor). Is anything left that could move one, and what must adoption say?

**Decision:**
1. *Lit-v1 §1 adopts the construction with one configuration, §9's* (configHash `17835aa2…77c1`), as pool-v3 §1 adopts v3:
   **E** names the construction and that hash, everything in lit-v1 is fixed by the name together with pool-v3's rules and
   the contracts' as lit-v1 reads them, including their stated limits; a later edit only corrects text; a backing declares
   `moe/lit/v1` only with that hash; adoption is not a deployment. Construction §C1.3 names lit-v1.
2. *One contract-text pin for both constructions.* Lit-v1 reads the contracts in the text pool-v3 §1 fixes (the revision
   that added §11.4, `e7f7f24`), whatever a later version changes in them. No contract file changed between `e7f7f24` and
   adoption, so the pin moves nothing. Construction's own numbered rules (C2b, C3, the invariants) stay unpinned by either
   construction, as for v3.
3. *Venue-ergo §1 names lit.* It enumerated pool-v3 and constructions whose venue records are kinds 1–3 only, so a strict
   reader could refuse lit terms naming an Ergo venue though lit-v1 §10 declared them admissible. §1 now admits a construction
   whose venue records are the kind 1–4 objects §6 attributes, such as lit-v1, where §8's publishing condition holds, and §8
   reads any configuration. No record, message, signature, attribution, index or answer changes, so the venue identity and
   every pool reader's verdict stand (a correction under venue-ergo §1's rule).
4. *Text fixes that change no byte or verdict:* §7, an acceptance owned by **K** carries two signatures by one key, each
   verified on its own (a hedged signer's two need not be equal; the code verifies each); §3, an exact replay's
   authorization has its kind's length (an operator answer; the store decodes before its lookup, as pool-v3's does); §1,
   the context prefix rule binds contexts declared later.
5. *Runtime:* nothing changes but the pin. Lit's readers already fix the domain (`litConfigHash`, checked against §9's
   frame at load), take no configuration or verifier identities, and refuse a served configuration other than §9's bytes;
   the reader's specification revision moves to the adopting revision (kept lit state is discarded once, as any code change
   does); "draft" leaves the code, command text and documents.

**Rationale:**
- *Adopt later*, after slice 12's replica or a venue drill of a lit journal on the testnet: neither reads or writes a lit
  byte the commands and the synthetic node do not already exercise, and the conformance read found nothing open; waiting
  only leaves lit backings undeclarable.
- *Pin the contracts at lit's own adopting revision:* two pins for one unchanged text, which could diverge if a later
  version amended a contract between them; reading pool-v3's pin keeps one text per contract for both constructions.
- *Pin Construction's rules too:* pool-v3 does not, and Construction changes are protocol decisions for every
  construction; a lit-only pin would split them. Stated as a limit.
- *Reword only lit-v1 §10 for the venue:* the strict reading sits in venue-ergo's own list, where an implementer of the
  venue profile looks.

**Evidence:** a fresh conformance read of lit-v1 at `a554f8a` against reference `c7418b8` ([archive](archive/2026-10-07-lit-v1-adoption-conformance-read.md))
recomputed every fixed length and identity from the text alone (node:crypto and noble Ed25519, no reference code) and
re-derived all vector records, outputs, signatures, the spent root, chains, fault evidence, header, trail, package, terms
and §8's derivations: no byte or verdict divergence; lit unit tests (89) pass. Its major (the contract pin, item 2) and
minors (item 3; transitions the vectors leave to the oracle tests: a settle's nullifiers in the spent root, a demand or
withdraw step's root, an opening over an imported closure, fault evidence at position 1, wallet vectors untied to record
vectors) and nits (item 4) are taken or recorded. A fresh review of the specification change found no blocker or major and no byte, identity or verdict change for
pool-v2, pool-v3 or lit readers (contracts unchanged since `e7f7f24`, configHash recomputed, 104 contexts scanned for prefix
pairs, links checked). Taken: §10 said the largest publication "fits one box", where §8 asks one transaction and the fit
depends on the location tree (now: wherever pool-v3's 15,498-byte release fits, and venue-ergo §1 admits a venue only where
§8 holds); §3's replay ignores the rest of the authorization (a settlement's carries its deadline too, so "signatures" was
narrow); venue-ergo §1's new sentence excludes pool-v3, which its own sentence governs; §5's merged run names the
construction's own frame. Its note that the reference guard checks every construction's venue against pool-v3's longest
publication (stricter than lit needs, within the reference's own venues) is now stated in `guard.ts`.

**Limits:** lit-v1 §12's cost estimates stay unmeasured (its falsifying measurement is unrun); the vectors bind every layout but not the transitions above (code-generic and oracle-tested); the M14e
residuals (kept rows under a rewritten digest) and Next 4's lit items (an), (ao) are implementation limits, not verdicts.
No lit backing has run on a live venue: the lit command drill runs on the synthetic node.

**Spec change:** [lit-v1 §1 and venue-ergo §§1, 8 at 80a4ea1](https://github.com/mediumofexchange/money-from-first-principles/blob/80a4ea1/lit-v1.md#1-status-and-scope) (money-from-first-principles #18), Construction §C1.3.

## 2026-10-07 — Retire the store checks' live testnet modes: the command drill is the live evidence (simplify)

**Status:** accepted (tooling; no runtime, specification or local/synthetic check change).

**Question:** `store-check.mjs --testnet` (the live journal) and `recovery-store-check.mjs`/`scope-store-check.mjs
--testnet --authorized-testnet` (live recovery, live two-backing scope, M8b) are probes that served live testnet
evidence. [M10d](#2026-10-06--drill-the-moe-commands-live-on-the-testnet-and-keep-the-testnet-context-without-a-difficulty-floor-slice-10-m10d)
(item 5) made the live command drill (`command-drill.mjs --testnet --authorized-testnet`) the current live evidence,
and their reports are historical: the live journal at `acc1ab7`, live recovery at `a72888b`, live scope at `8ca96cd`.
Should their live modes stay?

**Decision:**
1. *Retire the live modes.* `store-check.mjs --testnet` (its live venue, withheld-header reader, `testnet-reader.json`
   and public reader bundle), the recovery and scope checks' `--testnet --authorized-testnet`, `drill.mjs`'s testnet
   mode, `local-worker.mjs --testnet`, the live reader's selection and venue in `testnet.mjs`, the publication budget
   (`testnet-budget.mjs`) and `public-artifacts.mjs`, which only the live reader bundles used. Each check's local and
   `--ergo` modes keep their checks, report shapes and report paths.
2. *Keep the command drill's helpers.* `testnet.mjs` keeps what the command drill's live mode uses (node state, the
   retained wallet's key, plain transfers and confirmations) and takes the offline `--check` of the transfers, which
   stays in the real-proof list's `stores` group in place of `testnet-budget.mjs --check`.
3. *Cite the live journal report at its last revision.* `docs/pool-v3-testnet-verification.json` binds the retired
   path; it leaves `docs/` and is cited at
   [`09534a8`](https://github.com/mediumofexchange/reference-ts/blob/09534a8/docs/pool-v3-testnet-verification.json).
   The live recovery and scope reports stay cited at `a72888b` and `8ca96cd`.

**Rationale:**
- *Keeping them* costs about 400 lines of live harness (the live branches of four scripts, the live reader and the
  budget) kept true by nobody: they run only on the machine with the own testnet node and wallet, never in CI, so the
  journal and reader can drift from them unseen, and they duplicate the command drill's live path over the same venue.
- *Moving their checks onto the command drill* gains nothing: the command drill already publishes on the own testnet
  node, waits for inclusion and depth and reads with fresh processes, through the installed commands rather than a
  harness.
- A probe retires with the decision it served; M10d recorded that decision.

**Evidence:** the change removes 395 lines net (`testnet.mjs` 134, `store-check.mjs` 65, `drill.mjs` 41,
`testnet-budget.mjs` 95, the recovery and scope checks 32, `local-worker.mjs` 14, `public-artifacts.mjs` 14). The
local and `--ergo` modes' check labels, proof labels and report fields are unchanged (compared before and after);
`node scripts/pool/v3/testnet.mjs --check` passes offline; `test/real-proof-gate.test.ts` pins the list with it. The
local and synthetic reports bind changed sources and are re-recorded from CI's real-proof run.

**Limits:** live force and adoption (recovery) and the live two-backing scope are no longer re-runnable at current
code; their local and synthetic Ergo modes still run in CI, and the live command drill covers an offline operator's
return. The retained public bundles (`scratch/pool-v3-testnet-reader/`, `pool-v3-recovery-testnet-reader/`,
`pool-v3-scope-testnet-reader/`) re-read only with the scripts at `09534a8`.

**Spec change:** none needed.

## 2026-10-07 — One evidence chain for pool-v3 and lit, instantiated per construction (simplify)

**Status:** accepted after review (code only; no byte, identity or verdict change; specification unchanged).

**Question:** pool-v3 §7/§9 and lit-v1 §5/§6 define the same evidence chain (segment seed, evidence link, snapshot,
hash opening, portable fault-evidence frame) with lit's contexts, the evidence pair in place of the triple and lit's
target fields. The reference implemented it twice (`src/pool/v3/{commitments,fault-evidence}.ts` and
`src/lit/{commitments,fault-evidence}.ts`), M14a having copied the frames before lit reused any. Should one
implementation serve both before lit's adoption?

**Decision:**
1. `src/pool/evidence-chain.ts` (neutral core) implements the chain once; `evidenceChain(profile)` takes the contexts,
   the entry's digest names, the target fields, the field bound and whether an over-long fault-evidence frame is
   refused as a resource limit before it is copied. Each construction's `commitments.ts` instantiates it
   (`V3_CHAIN`, `LIT_CHAIN`) and re-exports the same names; history and receipt frames stay per construction (their
   fields differ).
2. pool-v3 keeps no pre-copy refusal (`boundBeforeCopy: false`, §9 as adopted, now pinned by a test); lit keeps its
   own (§6).
3. `Construction.capacity`, defined exactly where `namespace.tree` holds, is dropped: replay bounds a note-tree
   namespace by `NOTE_TREE_CAPACITY` (same `CAPACITY` verdict). Unused lit exports go.

**Rationale:** two copies of one frame drift apart and double what review must keep identical; lit-v1 states its
frames as pool-v3's with named substitutions, which one parameterised implementation mirrors. A shared class
hierarchy or construction branches inside one module were the alternatives; a profile object is smaller and keeps
each construction's bytes in its own module. Net −96 lines.

**Evidence:** one independent adversarial review compared the old and new modules side by side over about 115,000
cases (hostile byte arrays, throwing and changing getters, sparse and huge arrays, every truncation and single-bit
flip of frames under 1,000 bytes, every budget and position boundary, lit's pre-copy bound ±1): no difference in
bytes, decoded objects, true/false or error class for any single fault, and messages identical for decoded frames.
Findings: (1) where two faults occur at once in an in-process object (one field malformed, a later-read getter
throwing a non-encoding error), the first fault read now decides, since fields are read and checked in turn; the
old v3 and lit code differed from each other there too, no wire-derived value carries a getter, and the callers pass
owned or decoded values: accepted. (2) No test caught v3 gaining the pre-copy bound: one now does (it fails under that
mutation). (3) Reports re-recorded from CI run 37587854113 with unchanged check lists. (4) `segmentSeed` takes any
context: every caller passes a constant tag; kept. Unit suite 1,303 tests, `check:scripts`, `check:docs` pass;
CI's real-proof groups (replay included) pass.

**Spec change:** none needed.

## 2026-10-07 — Require the acceptance owner's signature in lit-v1, so K cannot answer a demand with the holder's own key (slice 14 M14g5)

**Status:** accepted after review; specification merged (money-from-first-principles #17, `a554f8a`); code in reference-ts
`claude/m14g5-acceptance-owner` (delivery is in WORK.md).

**Question:** Next 4 (am), from M14g2. In lit every owner key is public, and K sees the keys of a holder who presents to it:
the presenter key, the inputs' owners (the demand's openings), and keys the spend graph links to them, such as a change
key. Lit-v1 §4 had K sign the acceptance alone, its `owner` "a key K generates" by trust. K can therefore publish in time an
acceptance naming one of those holder keys. Settled, the output returns to the holder; left unreleased, C3.8 reads it as
the holder's lapse. K never takes the claims and is never dishonoured, which defeats the one consequence redemption has for
a backer. The wallet's `OWN_KEY` refusal (M14g2 item 7) protected only the holder's notes, not the reading. The
[direction of 2026-10-07](#2026-10-07--exercise-lits-failure-path-and-settle-7s-acceptance-owner-before-adoption-and-give-evidence-availability-a-replica-in-slice-12)
asks for this to be settled while lit-v1 is a draft, because adoption fixes its verdicts.

**Decision:**
1. *The owner key signs the acceptance too (lit-v1 §§3, 4, 7, 8).* `acceptanceBytes` is unchanged and signed by K and by the
   owner key it names. An acceptance is both signatures. The settle authorization is `u64 deadline || K's signature ||
   the owner's signature || release signature`, 200 bytes; the kind-2 publication body is `acceptanceBytes || K's
   signature || the owner's signature`, 253 bytes. Records stay 189–719 bytes, the largest publication 569 and the body bound 478.
2. *Read everywhere alike.* Admission, replay and venue force check both signatures (`recovery.ts`'s one settlement
   check through lit's view). C3.8 reads an acceptance, published or carried by a release, as an answer only where both
   verify (lit-v1 §7; the reference's lit C3.8 reading is M14g5b, and no lit read lists answers before it). A settlement has no compact intrinsic failure (§6): all three of its signatures are judged with its demand.
3. *K's wallet* signs with `acceptSecret`, derived again from the seed when it returns or publishes the acceptance (RFC 8032
   signatures are deterministic) and checked against the saved owner (`STORAGE` otherwise), so `backer_acceptances` keeps its schema. §8 adds that a wallet signs `acceptanceBytes`
   only as a backing's K, for a demand of that backing, with no key but its own K and that demand's `acceptSecret`
   (the reference's `accept` already requires the demand's backing to be the terms').
4. *The holder's settle* refuses an acceptance its owner key did not sign (`INVALID`). No acceptance K can make names a key
   of the holder, so the `OWN_KEY` scan of the wallet's owner keys and of every presenter key it saved is removed; `OWN_KEY`
   stays for an issue to the wallet's own key.

**Alternatives:**
- *(a) Read an acceptance naming a key a reader can tell is the holder's (presenter, input owners) as no answer:* no byte
  change, but it misses change keys and any other key K links through the public spend graph, so the escape remains.
- *(b') The owner's signature on the published acceptance only, the settlement unchanged (136 bytes):* it closes the lapse
  reading, and a settled demand needs the holder's release. But that consent then depends on the holder's wallet recognising
  every key of its own, and a seed-restored wallet that has not yet read the backing whose key K names refuses nothing (M14g2's
  recorded limit; an output of another backing to one of its keys is still its note). It also needs two acceptance shapes and
  a C3.8 reading that differs by evidence path. (b) costs 64 bytes and one verification per settlement only (redemptions,
  not payments) and is one rule for every reading.
- *(d) Settle to K's own key, no owner field:* removes a derivation and 96 bytes, but puts every redeemed claim under the
  issuing key, which must then sign each of their spends or burns and may be an external signer. (b) still lets K choose
  owner = K (two equal signatures).
- *A public-key tweak (owner = K + H(demand, deadline)·B):* no owner field or signature, but a new primitive and a signer
  outside RFC 8032.
- *Defer as a stated limit:* rejected by the 2026-10-07 direction.

**Evidence and limits:**
- `test/lit-records.test.ts`: the 200-byte authorization and the 253-byte publication are checked against the independent
  oracle, and `lit-v1-vectors.json` is re-recorded at `a554f8a` (it now carries both acceptance signatures). An acceptance
  carrying K's signature alone (189 bytes) fails to decode (`truncated`), and one past 253 bytes as `field too long`.
- `test/lit-state.test.ts`: a settlement is refused `SIGNATURE` in replay where the owner signature is K's, where the owner is
  the holder's input key or the presenter key and K signed for it, and where a stranger signed for the presenter key; under
  force where K signed for the holder's input key, the presenter key or another key; owner = K has force.
- `test/lit-wallet.test.ts`: the backer's acceptance verifies under its owner, and its published kind 2 decodes to it. The holder's settle refuses acceptances
  naming its input's owner, the demand's presenter, or another standing demand's presenter, and a zero owner signature,
  each as `INVALID: the acceptance's owner key did not sign it`, and one K did not sign as the obligor refusal.
- Run passes: the lit command drill (`check:lit:commands`, 143 processes: accept, relay, settle and the gap route) and the
  unit suite (84 files, 1,301 tests).
- `src/lit/construction.ts`'s reader specification moves to `lit-v1 a554f8a`, so no kept lit state from the older rule is reused.
- Limit: no lit backing is deployed (lit-v1 is a draft), so no record of the 136-byte shape needs reading.
- Observation for a later pool version (pool-v3 is fixed): K knows the owner values it issued to, so where it can link a
  demand to such a holder it can name one, and a holder that does not release reads as lapsed. It is weaker than lit's case
  (the pool hides which notes a demand names). A v4 fix would prove the owner secret in the acceptance.

**Review:** one fresh adversarial reviewer read the specification change (`695f1da`) with these alternatives. It
found no break in the rule, checking:
- every key class K can see;
- domain separation against `statementBytes` and `releaseBytes`, and the lit-only HKDF roots;
- small-order, non-canonical and mixed-order keys under the strict rule;
- owner = K, malleability, and a colluding third party.

It verified every size. Two majors, both resolved in `2b416e8`:
- §6's intrinsic reading of a bad owner signature was ambiguous between its two sentences. It is now explicit: none for a settlement.
- (b') was not compared. Now compared above; the reviewer accepted keeping (b) on the restored-wallet reason.

Three minors, resolved:
- force did not name the owner check;
- §8 lacked the wallet's signing rule (its wording completed in `50fa587` after read-back);
- extensions over-claimed generation where possession is checked.

Nits taken: §12's cite and a falsifier, wording, owner = K. A read-back confirmed each fix.

A second fresh adversarial reviewer read the code patch (`3c78c3e`). It found no blocker or major and
confirmed the codec, every verdict path, wallet re-derivation and pool-v3 byte identity. It also ran a force probe for every key class.
- Minor, current v3 reports drifted: `wallet-store.ts` and `construction.ts` are bound sources. They are re-recorded from the PR's CI artifact.
- Minor, no force test: one is added (above).
- Minor, no unit test of lit `publishAcceptance`: one is added (above).
- Nits taken:
  - the derived owner signature is checked against the saved owner;
  - the owner signature reads its domain from the acceptance alone;
  - the settle docstring is corrected;
  - item 2 names M14g5b.
- Noted: lit state saved under the 136-byte shape does not decode; no lit backing is deployed.

**Spec change:** [lit-v1 at a554f8a](https://github.com/mediumofexchange/money-from-first-principles/blob/a554f8a/lit-v1.md) (§§3, 4, 6, 7, 8, 12) and [extensions](https://github.com/mediumofexchange/money-from-first-principles/blob/a554f8a/extensions.md#the-transparent-profile).

## 2026-10-07 — Run lit backings through the moe commands, each directory declaring its construction (slice 14 M14g4)

**Status:** accepted after review; delivery is in WORK.md.

**Question:** M14f–M14g3 put a lit scope in the one journal, lit notes and acts in the one wallet, and lit records and
receipts on the one service wire, each with the construction as an option. The `moe` commands still opened pool-v3
journals, wallets, evidence files and service clients only. The [direction of 2026-10-07](#2026-10-07--exercise-lits-failure-path-and-settle-7s-acceptance-owner-before-adoption-and-give-evidence-availability-a-replica-in-slice-12)
accepts M14g4 by a command drill of a lit backing on the synthetic node, its failure path included. Where does a command
learn which construction to open, and what does a lit directory keep?

**Decision:**
1. *One construction per directory, declared at init.* `init` of a wallet, operator or reader takes
   `--construction moe/pool/v3` (the default) or `moe/lit/v1` and keeps it in config.json. A directory made before the
   field existed is pool-v3's, so existing directories open unchanged. Every file a directory holds already serves one
   construction: the journal, wallet and evidence files each name their configuration domain, and `serve` owns one
   journal. Terms, journal, wallet, service client and reads therefore all use the declared construction.
   `terms add`, `terms create` and `open` authenticate terms under it (codec, name, signature and configuration domain).
   Terms that decode under the other construction are refused as `CONSTRUCTION`. `restore` and `restore-seed` take the
   flag, and the library refuses a handoff of the other construction (its profile and domain differ).
2. *No parameters where nothing is proved.* A directory whose construction carries no proofs (lit) keeps no proving
   parameters and opens no prover or verifier. `--parameters` is refused before the directory exists. The relay
   verifies nothing, so it keeps none either (the pool-v3 drill's relay init drops the flag). `relay publish` reads the
   publication in whichever construction's frame it decodes. It requires the configuration domain the publication
   names to be that construction's, and it takes a demand's instant for `EARLY` from that construction's view. A relay
   is a third party's service, so it serves either construction.
3. *The lit wallet's commands.*
   - `request` writes lit-v1 §8's frame (`moe/wallet/lit/v1/request`: backing, quantity and a fresh owner key). `pay`
     and `issue` authenticate that frame by its digest.
   - `fulfill` and `fulfillment` print the credited output and the request's key.
   - `accept` names `acceptSecret`'s key and writes lit's kind-2 publication, which `settle --acceptance` reads.
   - `terms create` writes lit terms. Their silence clause is the duration alone, so `--challenge` is a usage error.
   - `move-window` takes M14g2's §8 window move. A wallet restored from its seed needs it before its first request.
   - `freshen` refuses as `CONSTRUCTION` (M14g2 item 5). `presentation`, in the wallet and the reader, also refuses as
     `CONSTRUCTION` until M14g5 reads C3.8 for lit.
   - Every other command (issue, pay, sync, demand, withdraw, settle, burn, submit, status, publish, handoff, restore,
     restore-seed, return, adopt, supply) runs unchanged over the declared construction.
   - The explanations a lit directory prints say what lit-v1 §11 says: every statement's backings, values, owner keys
     and spend graph are public.
4. *A fix found by the drill.* The reader's `supply` listed each forced publication's kind as pool-v3's
   `record.kind`. It printed nothing for a lit record (a lit record carries its kind in its statement). It now reads
   the kind through the construction.

**Alternatives:**
- *Dispatch per backing from the terms' tag 5, one directory holding both constructions:* a wallet directory would need
  a wallet file per construction, each with its own seed (two seeds, two backups and a `seed --show` naming either),
  or a lit seed derived from the pool's (a new custody derivation). An operator directory would need a journal and a
  `serve` per construction. Holding both kinds of backing in one place is not worth that cost; two directories do it.
- *A lit command (`moe lit ...`) beside `moe`:* a second set of roles, files and refusals over the same library.
- *Inferring the construction from the files already present:* a fresh directory has none until its first command,
  and the first terms kept would silently decide.
- *Keeping parameters in a lit directory, unused:* about 2 MiB of G1 points fetched over the network or copied at
  every init for nothing, a network dependency a lit init otherwise lacks; the relay's likewise.

**Evidence and limits:** `scripts/lit/command-drill.mjs` (`check:lit:commands`, now in `check:scripts`) runs the
built `moe` as fresh processes over the synthetic node. It took 1 min 42 s for 144 processes (Intel Xeon @ 2.10GHz).
- A lit backer's wallet creates terms, the operator opens and serves them, and a reader keeps them.
- The backer issues to a holder's owner-key request, and the holder fulfills it.
- The holder pays a shop with change. The shop fulfills, and a rerun exits 4.
- The shop demands, the backer accepts and a relay publishes the acceptance. The shop settles, the backer finds the
  settled note and burns it, and the reader's supply reads 10 issued, 3 burned.
- A withdrawn demand's presented note pays another invoice.
- With `serve` stopped past the silence, the holder demands from its kept package and publishes through the relay.
  Its sync reads the demand locked by force with the gap open, and the reader reads it as one forced kind-4
  publication from a package file. The backer's acceptance and the holder's settlement then go through the relay and
  read final by force. `return` and `adopt` run, and after adoption the backer holds the settled note.
- A handoff restores the shop's wallet with its fulfillments. A seed restoration finds the same holdings and refuses a
  request (`WINDOW`) until `move-window` is final, after which it requests.
- Hostile cases: pool-v3 terms into a lit wallet and a lit handoff restored as pool-v3 (`CONSTRUCTION`, no directory left), a pool-v3 request frame under its own digest (`REQUEST`), `--parameters`
  to a lit or relay init and `--challenge` to lit terms (usage), an unknown construction, freshen and presentation
  (`CONSTRUCTION`), a relay file for another venue (`VENUE`), and a move with a window still open (`CONFLICT`).
- No lit process loads a `@noir-lang` module.
- `test/cli.test.mjs` adds four tests: terms under each construction, the other's refused by name, and lit's frame
  naming pool-v3's configuration refused as `CONFIGURATION`; the relay's reading (a lit acceptance, another
  configuration, garbage); a lit and a pool-v3 demand's instant; and config.json's construction (declared, absent,
  unknown).
- The unit suite (1,297 tests before these) passes. The pool-v3 command drill, with real proofs, runs in CI.

Limits:
- The drill runs the checkout's build, not a packed install (the pool-v3 drill covers packaging), and only on the
  synthetic node, not live.
- A reader has no kept-package fallback when the operator does not answer: it reads in a gap only from a package file.
  The slice 12 replica serves that case.
- C3.8's lit reading is M14g5.

**Review:** an independent adversarial review of the patch (65ff770) found no blocker or major.
- What it checked: cross-construction terms, requests, acceptances, wallets, journals and handoffs; the relay's
  reading under 80,000 mutated lit and pool-v3 publications (every refusal named); and pool-v3's verdicts and outputs.
  For pool-v3, `actOut`'s deadline and the relay's instant are the same public inputs as before, and the force kind is
  unchanged.
- Four minors, all taken:
  - A lit `demand` told the holder to freshen, which a lit wallet refuses. Its notes now say what a lit demand
    discloses, and the drill checks them.
  - A handoff restored under the other construction was refused as `INVALID` and left a directory without config.json
    behind. The handoff is now opened before the directory exists, and that case is refused as `CONSTRUCTION`; the drill
    checks the refusal and that no directory is left.
  - A pool-v3 reader's config.json edited to lit, over an existing evidence.db, exited 3 with an unnamed error. It now
    refuses as `CONSTRUCTION`. The wallet and journal files were already refused by name (`CONFLICT`, `STORAGE`).
  - The relay's `EARLY` instant was untested. A unit test now reads a lit and a pool-v3 demand's instant.
- Nits taken:
  - The drill's pool-v3 frame is a real one under its own digest.
  - `restore-seed` checks `--construction` and `--parameters` before reading stdin.
  - `move-window` prints the saved payment's `kind` as `status` does.
- Noted: for pool directories the relay now checks `CONFIGURATION` before `SUBJECT`, and three refusal messages name the
  construction; no refusal code changed. The pool-v3 command drill and every real-proof group passed in CI on 65ff770
  (run 37565053553).

**Spec change:** none needed (the directory's construction and its files are the reference's application layout).

## 2026-10-07 — Exercise lit's failure path and settle §7's acceptance owner before adoption, and give evidence availability a replica in slice 12

**Status:** accepted (order and scope of WORK.md's Next; no specification or code change).

**Question:** since the [previous direction check](#2026-10-03--measure-the-design-point-at-sizes-a-run-can-prove-and-give-the-holders-transport-and-funding-a-slice-before-release-assurance)
(reference-ts e2c503d), slice 11 and M10d closed, the [claim-layer direction](#2026-10-05--build-extensions-claim-layer-profiles-beside-the-shielded-pool-each-chosen-per-backing)
put lit notes before release assurance, and slice 14 delivered M14a–M14g3 (PRs #116–#127; `lit-v1.md` drafted and
clarified in specification PRs #12–#16). M14g4 (the commands) and M14h (adoption) remain. Is the path still the shortest
to the release target, and does any release gate lack a slice?

**Findings:**
- **Slice 14 reuses rather than adds.** Lit runs through the one state machine, reader, journal, wallet and service wire
  behind a construction seam (about 1,600 lines in `src/lit/`), pool-v3's verdicts unchanged at each step. Each
  milestone's adversarial review still finds real defects (M14f a blocker, M14g2 three majors), so review per milestone
  keeps paying for itself.
- **Adoption would fix a verdict that is still in question.** Adoption fixes every byte, identity and verdict of
  `moe/lit/v1` (lit-v1 §1); a later change is `moe/lit/v2` by successor. Next 4 (am) records that K, who sees a lit
  holder's owner keys, can publish in time an acceptance naming one of them: the wallet refuses to settle it (`OWN_KEY`),
  yet C3.8 reads the demand as answered, so K escapes dishonour. lit-v1 §7 currently says C3.4's distinct acceptance
  owner "is not needed". This is a verdict rule, cheap to settle while the text is a draft. A reader can tell only the
  presenter and input owner keys; K may also link a holder's change key, so the rule is not yet chosen: reading such an
  acceptance as no answer, or requiring the owner key's signature in the acceptance (a byte change).
- **Lit's failure path is not yet exercised end to end.** Next 4 (an): no lit gap-route act through the wallet, and no
  lit `presentation` (C3.8's reading is pool-v3's only); M14f's journal test adopts a forced lit demand, but no lit backing
  has run on a venue (M14f limits: "no venue drill of a lit journal"). The direction's design item 2 mapped
  compartmentalized failure and recovery for each profile; pool-v3 was adopted only after its failure paths ran, once
  nothing left could move a byte.
- **One release gate has no slice.** "Available, recoverable state" ends "Durable evidence availability remains open",
  and the release contract asks for evidence "from independently operated sources". Only the operator's `serve`
  supplies evidence; a wallet in a gap reads its own kept package, and a holder who missed the last sync has no other
  source. C2b.3.3 has the trail come from replicas, the [M10b decision](#2026-10-02--install-one-moe-command-over-role-directories-on-ergo-venues-only-with-keys-in-files-and-funding-apart-from-the-wallet-slice-10-m10b)
  assigned a replica role to this gate, and no Next item or slice took it. The visibility matrix names syncing from a
  replica as one way to break the operator's join of address and backing, which is slice 12's duty 3.
- **Two design-point measurements have no owner either.** The operator's own memory at depth (Next 5) is unmeasured,
  and the wallet's first sync and steady state at 10⁵ were lost to a container restart (flat to 10⁴); an unmeasured item
  fails the "Practical deployment" gate, whose standing overstated the second. The 10⁶ run, a local-machine item since
  the 2026-10-03 direction, was cited as an Open question that WORK.md did not hold.
- **Smaller drift:** the production requirements still call the reference's transparent implementation retired (the
  account ledger is; lit notes are being built), and Next 7's proposed AGENTS.md wording on real-proof job times was
  left unapplied.

**Decision:**
1. **Slice 14 keeps its stop (adoption) and gains its failure path first.** M14g4: the `moe` dispatch on the terms'
   construction, accepted by a command drill of a lit backing on the synthetic node: issue, request, pay, sync, demand,
   accept, settle, withdraw, burn and a reader's supply, then the gap route with the operator stopped (a demand
   published through the relay, its force read) and a return and adoption. M14g5: settle (am) as a lit-v1 draft
   clarification (spec first, fresh review, C0a; the rule open between the two above), then lit `presentation` with
   C3.8's reading. M14h: adoption, once
   nothing left can move a byte or a verdict.
2. **Slice 12 becomes the holder's transport, funding and evidence sources.** Besides duties 2–3 (the SOCKS5 probe
   first), it adds a replica: an evidence-only role (for example `moe reader serve`) serving what a reader has kept and
   verified through the one service wire's evidence read, admitting nothing and signing nothing. The operator-offline
   drill then syncs a wallet from the replica. The evidence read is loopback-only today with a service-wide bearer
   token, so slice 12 decides, with duty 3's credential, whether a replica's read is public and token-free or takes a
   shared, non-identifying credential (Construction: evidence retrievable by a stranger). This closes the gate's
   "independently operated sources" for the smallest profile with no new protocol mechanism; durable storage
   qualification stays with release assurance.
3. **Order: slice 14, then 12, then 13.** One design-point rerun in slice 13 at the latest measures the operator's memory
   at depth and the wallet's 10⁵ points; the gate's standing now names both. The 10⁶ run is a non-blocking
   local-machine Open question. The Bitcoin probe (Next 12) stays read-only before slice 13 and off the critical path.
4. **Corrections:** the production requirements' release contract names lit notes as built beside the pool, and
   AGENTS.md takes Next 7's real-proof wording.
5. The estimate moves to about **65% (55–74%)**: the replica is remaining work not counted before, and slice 14 gains
   a milestone.

**Rationale:**
- *Adopt lit-v1 after M14g4 as planned:* (am) would then be a `moe/lit/v2` change, and any byte the failure path's drill
  moves too. Settling it now costs one draft clarification.
- *Defer (am) as a stated limit:* it lets the backer escape the one consequence redemption has for it, which is the
  purpose of C3.8's record; a limit is the wrong home for it.
- *A replica in release assurance, or after release:* the gate cannot pass without it; slice 12 already decides where
  holders sync from, and both serve the same read, so one slice decides it once.
- *A new replica protocol:* the service wire's evidence read already serves exactly what a reader verifies, from any
  source (pool-v3's packages are source-independent), so reuse adds no mechanism.
- *A static mirror of whole packages,* which `--package <file>` already reads: each sync would move the whole history
  (about 15.6 GB of records at the design point) where the wire's `after=` read serves only what is new.
- *Drop lit before release to reach release sooner:* the claim-layer direction set its timing and it is two milestones
  from adoption; dropping it now would discard reviewed work for little time saved.
- No line of work is dropped.

**Evidence:** WORK.md and the log at fc6b27d (reference-ts e2c503d..fc6b27d, 163 commits without merges: 13 `feat`,
47 `fix`, 80 `docs` (16 of them report re-recordings), 13 `test`, 9 `chore`, 1 `refactor`; specification 6cbd9bc..3af6abe; site c27099c..dfc298c; orgprofile
d321445..ad8e695); lit-v1 §§1, 7; the M14f and M14g2 decisions' limits; the production requirements' gates; `moe`'s
usage text (no evidence-serving role besides `operator serve`). CI on main fc6b27d green. No new measurement.
Documentation only. A fresh reviewer verified the factual claims and found one major (the wallet's lost 10⁵ points and
the 10⁶ run had no owner; items 3 and the gate's standing now cover them), two minors (the replica's credential, item 2;
(am)'s rule left open, item 1) and three nits (the static-mirror reason, these counts, wording), all taken.

**Spec change:** none needed; M14g5's clarification follows the decision rule.

## 2026-10-07 — Serve a lit journal through the one service wire and client, the construction an option (slice 14 M14g3)

**Status:** accepted after review; delivery is in WORK.md.

**Question:** M14f put a lit scope in the one operator journal and M14g1–g2 put lit notes and acts in the one wallet, but the
local service (`service-wire.ts`, `service-http.ts`, `service-client.ts`) still decoded pool-v3 records, pool-v3's 355-byte
receipts and pool-v3 packages only, and its client pinned the adopted pool-v3 domain. A lit journal could not be served, nor a
lit wallet submit or sync through the client. How do the wire, server and client serve a lit scope without a second service?

**Decision:**
1. *One profile, the construction read from its owner.* The envelope (`pool-store/v3`) and the served frame are application
   framing that sign nothing; every record, receipt and package they carry names its configuration domain. So one profile
   serves both constructions: the server reads commands and replies through its journal's construction (`journal.construction`,
   now public), and the client through the construction its caller expects (`ServiceIdentity.construction`, pool-v3's by
   default), whose configuration is the domain it pins. A client and service of different constructions refuse each other's
   records, receipts and evidence by decoding or by the pinned domain, before anything is kept.
2. *A receipt codec per construction.* `JournalFrames.receipts` (decode, encode, and verify under an authority whose scope root
   is pool-v3's and absent for lit, lit-v1 §5) is what a submitter reads of the receipt the journal writes. It replaces the lit
   wallet's own `KeyedWalletFrames.receipt`. The wire checks a reply's receipt is canonical under it (bounded at 512 bytes; the
   decoder takes the exact length); the client verifies it in the record's segment and scope and binds the statement identity,
   as for pool-v3 (an exact retry may carry another proof or authorization and still gets the original receipt).
3. *The wallet's one receipt check.* The wallet's two receipt paths become one over the codec and the construction's view and
   digests: the receipt must be the operator's in the record's segment and scope and bind the saved statement, proof digest
   (pool-v3's; a lit receipt names none, read as the empty digest lit's reader keeps) and signature digest. Pool-v3's verdicts
   are unchanged (the view's segment, scope and identity and the reader's digests are exactly what it read); a service answer
   that is not a receipt object is now `INVALID` for pool-v3 too, as it already was for lit.
4. *Not here.* The `moe` commands still open pool-v3 journals and wallets (M14g4 dispatches on the terms' construction).

**Alternatives:**
- *A lit profile (`lit-store/v1`) and served frame of its own:* a second name for the same framing; the domain already separates
  the constructions, and a mismatched pair fails at the first decode.
- *The server taking a construction option beside its journal:* the two could disagree; the journal's own is the one it judges by.
- *Keeping the wallet's two receipt paths:* one rule written twice, the general codec beside the special case.

**Evidence and limits:** `test/lit-service.test.ts` (four tests): a lit journal behind `createV3Service`; K's issue admitted through
the client with lit-v1's 290-byte receipt and its exact replay; commit, publish, a whole package and an incremental sync into a
lit evidence store; a pool-v3 client refusing a lit record and lit evidence, the lit service refusing a pool-v3 record (`INVALID`),
a lit request refused on the wire and by the client, another operator's client refusing commitments and evidence; receipts for
another statement, segment, domain or signer refused; each codec refusing the other's receipt and an authority of the other shape;
a lit wallet funded, paying and credited with every read supplied through the client, refusing forged receipts (`INVALID`). Eight
guards were removed in turn: each failed a test except pool-v3's codec scope-root guard, which `verifyReceipt` already refuses
(kept for its type). The pool-v3 service, wallet and payer suites pass with the tests' mocks naming their construction. Limits: no
process-level lit service drill (`check:pool:v3-service` stays pool-v3's); the commands are M14g4.

**Review:** an independent adversarial review of the patch, with probes (each codec against the other's receipts, 25,728 mutated
lit records through the view and digests, hostile records over the real lit service), found no blocker or major: no
cross-construction acceptance, the wallet's check equivalent for pool-v3 (its decoder always yields a proof digest) and at least
as strict for lit, receipts canonical at their exact length, every decodable record answered by a named code. Its three minors
are taken: negative tests now assert each refusal's reason; a lit record of another domain is refused by the server's domain
check before the journal (`INVALID`, nothing admitted); the lit wallet refuses forged statement, domain, operator and pool-v3
receipts. Its nit on `sync` is taken: an evidence store of another construction is refused by name before any request. Not
taken: a startup check that a journal-like object names a construction (the typed journal always does), and a cheaper domain read
than the view (a few hashes per submission, behind the wallet credential).

**Spec change:** none needed (the service envelope is the reference's application framing).

## 2026-10-06 — Exclude a continuation whose opening the record moved past, and read a receipt's term end without its segment's opening (audit, area 31)

**Status:** accepted. This is the rolling audit of the v3 multi-backing and recovery readers (`scope-reader.ts`,
`package-reader.ts`, `scope-evidence.ts`, `receipt-state.ts`, `fault-observer.ts`, `non-service.ts`,
`authorization-evidence.ts`, `dishonour.ts`). It supersedes, for the runtime reader, the unheld-opening reading of
[2026-09-19](2026-09.md#2026-09-19--import-the-exact-single-backing-finalized-closure-in-local-replay). No
specification change.

**Question:** do the readers reach the verdict an honest independent reader must reach, on complete and on hostile
evidence, against pool-fault C2.10.11, pool-authority C2.10.9a–b, pool-recovery C2b.3–C2b.6 and C3.8, and
pool-v3 §§7.1, 9, 12–14? An independent audit found two places where complete evidence gave no verdict:
- A held continuation whose segment's opening sequence the record does not hold was unresolved. Its own higher
  sequence is held, so the record moved past the opening (Construction C2.3.3: "a sequence the record stepped over
  is one it never held"). No later evidence can change that, yet C2.10.11's unresolved class is the one "evidence
  obtained later resolves". Every later read of the scoped backings refused for good: snapshot, clock, count, a
  successor's takeover and the operator's own repair opening. So an operator could freeze a backing for good by
  withholding its own opening.
- A receipt walk threw unresolved unless the receipt's segment opening was held and not lapsed. That covered a
  receipt whose `after` the record moved past (C2.10.9b: "lapsed where the record moved past `after`") and a segment
  whose opening was witnessed after a scoped term ended (C2.10.9b: "Checkpoints witnessed at or after the earliest
  term end are lapsed for the whole scope").

**Decision:**
1. `judge` excludes such a continuation with check `OPENING`, because its history extends no last valid prefix
   (C2.10.11's excluded class).
   - Lapse is judged first. A scoped backing's gap that is open at the continuation's index needs no opening, so
     the continuation is lapsed there. Only the silence boundary waits for an opening.
   - The same order now covers a held opening sequence that names another segment.
   - The lapse carries the gap's clock record read at the continuation's index.
   - The existing full-trail requirement before an exclusion stands.
2. A receipt walk applies a scoped term's end whether or not its segment opened. Only the silence boundary waits for
   the opening as witnessed (C2b.4.3). A segment whose opening the record never held reads moved-past, repair,
   abandoned or pending in C2.10.9a–b's order instead of refusing. A lapsed opening always meets the term boundary
   first, because only a term end lapses an opening (C2.10.11).
3. The receipt loop starts at the header's sequence instead of scanning the operator's commitments from zero.

**Rationale:** the 2026-09-19 choice kept an unheld opening unresolved so that a local experiment could not
"descend to older state". The runtime reader reads a record authenticated complete through its judging index
(C2.10.13), and every receipt and clock verdict already relies on that (moved-past, holes). Below a held sequence,
"not held" is a fact of that record. A segment that never opened has no valid checkpoint: no reader at any index
judged its continuations valid, and their receipts read lapsed or abandoned, never final. So passing them rolls back
nothing that was finalized. The other options:
- Keeping unresolved preserves a permanent, operator-chosen freeze, which breaks compartmentalized failure.
- Reading the continuation as lapsed would stretch C2.10.11's lapse rule, which names only term ends and silence,
  beyond its text.
The harness cases that asserted the old reading (`local-check.mjs` silence group, `import-check.mjs` missing-opening
case) now assert the exclusion. In the second case A's next opening imports its own last valid state.

**Evidence:**
- Four tests in `test/pool-v3-scope-reader.test.ts` fail on main and pass with the fix: continuation excluded and
  repair valid; receipt lapsed moved-past; receipt abandoned at the next scope change; receipt lapsed at the scope
  boundary.
- A fifth test passes with the review fix and fails without it: the same unopened continuation is lapsed in an
  open gap and excluded outside one.
- Unit suite (1265 tests) and typecheck pass at 7843318.
- `check:scripts` passes at 7843318.
- `check:pool:local-replay` passes at 7843318: 223 groups, 65 real proofs.

Review: an independent adversarial review of the patch confirmed the rollback argument; it tried every reader index,
the receipt walk, wallet, redemption and settlement paths. It found one defect, which is fixed: the first commit
excluded an unopened continuation before judging an open gap, where C2.10.11 gives lapsed. A read-back confirmed the fix. It found no stale clock state from the clock read at the continuation's index: a genuine opening at that index computes the same clock. On its advice the lapse carries that clock record (C2b.4.1). The gap also lapses a continuation whose trail is withheld, since lapse needs no trail.

Its remaining findings are recorded here:
- The argument assumes the venue answer is complete and final through the judging index (C2.10.13, §13.2).
  This adds no trust: a venue omitting b0, b1 and b2 together already let an opening import a1 on main.
- C2b.4.3's "the opening and the complete scope must be authenticated" is read as governing the silence boundary
  alone. C2b.4.1 defines that boundary only for a witnessed opening. A receipt whose `after` is an excluded
  continuation of a segment that never opened therefore stays pending until the operator's next scope change or a
  term end, since no silence boundary exists for that segment. This is a candidate for a later specification
  clarification; it is no worse than the permanent refusal it replaces.
- An exclusion or lapse of such a continuation still needs its served trail. A withheld trail leaves it unresolved,
  so the backing resumes only once the operator serves that trail. This availability limit was already recorded at
  2026-09. Likewise a held opening whose snapshot is withheld refuses before the gap test.
- A moved-past `after` reads before abandonment, as [Construction §C2.10](https://github.com/mediumofexchange/money-from-first-principles/blob/df2332a/construction.md)'s
  moved-past answer and the 2026-09 receipt reading have it.

Deferred to WORK.md Next 4, with exact fixes:
- (ag) The package reader authenticates the selection's scope from the selected backing's snapshot, not the
  directory's first entry (pool-v3 §7.1). A sibling snapshot naming an unknown segment then reads unresolved for
  that backing where the first backing's read refuses `SNAPSHOT`.
- (ah) `judge` resolves the reader's own snapshot before lapse. A term-lapsed checkpoint whose own snapshot
  preimage is withheld is unresolved where C2.10.11 reads term lapse from the header and scope alone. This was noted
  at 2026-09 and persists.
- (ai) A committed snapshot preimage that hashes to its digest but does not decode surfaces as `EncodingError`.
- (aj) The non-service count decodes every publication of the backing and keeps every request. Keep identity → first
  index for entries before the window.
- (ak) The replay identity includes the selected backing, so a multi-backing checkpoint read for its second backing
  replays the segment again.
- Already recorded:
  - (r) covers CONTEXT and TERMS_CONTEXT before lapse.
  - The 2026-09 local-replay review noted `IMPORT_RANK` in `judge` as unreachable given C2.3.3 and disjoint terms.
    It is kept as a guard.

The audit found these sound:
- Rank order and predecessor selection, prefix merging, adoption indices and block derivation.
- Silence clock arithmetic, force, and the running force state.
- The receipt walk's precedence and hole counting.
- Scope authentication by name hash.
- Fault and authorization facts: no fact can be raised against an honest operator.
- The C3.8 reading: prospective void, timely acceptances, taken releases.
- The C2b.5.2 count window and first identity index.
